### Design and invariants

1. Current-only takes an explicit `BE_DELIVERY_SCHEMA_SHA256` approved baseline; no fallback to the artifact's own digest. Thus the separately activated TARGET can be approved without deleting checksum enforcement. The candidate still proves exact current history before promotion; this mode has no DB credential or runner call. OLD remains the immutable initial-activation input, not the eternal routine-delivery baseline.
2. Versioned migration is a distinct routine mode. A public, digest-approved packet binds revision/tree, exact prior checksum manifest, target checksum manifest, target project, predecessor deployment/source/artifact/configuration and compatibility/recovery approval hashes. Durable authorization binds the independent successful checks job's exact artifact digest; no pre-build artifact digest must be guessed. Require an unchanged proper prefix, at least the already activated three-file history, and exact sealed target assets. Initial OLD→TARGET is rejected here: roles, Owner bootstrap and configuration phases remain exclusively activation.
3. The migration packet also binds the separately supplied migration-input digest. Reuse `parseMigrationConfig`, verified Session TLS, the existing runner/status/report and packaged operator. No new migration history, runtime DDL, role provisioning, Auth operation or production configuration mutation.
4. Use the same production concurrency and GitHub deployment journal as current-only/activation. Split public/provider authorization, SQL-only execution and provider delivery into separate workflow steps. Before SQL, reserve the durable intent and confirm its exact packet/artifact/source binding remotely, attest the still-bound compatible predecessor metadata and protected runtime configuration, and persist an exclusive local authorization. The SQL worker receives only migration inputs; no Vercel/GitHub/runtime/Owner credentials.
5. The worker persists `started/unknown` before invoking the runner once; exact prehistory, checksum identity, confirmed commit, per-file outcomes, durable report and exact postflight are mandatory. Later delivery verifies the same authorization/outcome and remote intent before creation. Failed/unknown commit, lost receipt/status acknowledgement, changed main/alias and failed candidate validation stop without SQL/create replay. Confirmed SQL followed by deployment failure remains committed and fenced; never down, automatic rollback or fallback to699.
6. Compatibility is an explicit reviewed predecessor approval, not inferred from an append-only migration filename. Bind its exact deployed source/artifact and runtime configuration to approved compatibility and recovery evidence; verify those identities and readiness before SQL and again after migration. A future transition with no genuinely compatible predecessor is blocked until a separately reviewed compatible release exists. This increment does not invent one or reuse the one-time bridge for arbitrary future histories.
7. Preserve exact tested-artifact transfer, public tool bootstrap before credentials, icn1, one-shot official CLI, known-ID-before-inspection, full upload attestation, private candidate checks and same-ID final-domain checks. No provider settings change. Make workflow gating/configuration prerequisites visible in a secret-free summary.

Source inspection also found `parseConfig` applying the one-time full TARGET assertion to every enabled application runtime. Routine strict runtime must preserve the immutable activated three-file prefix while allowing reviewed later versions; strict readiness still requires the entire compiled ordered history. Keep the one-time bridge and activation packet pinned to exact OLD/TARGET, never widen them. Add a focused boundary regression; no migration file or public API contract changes.

### Technical context and constitution check

Keep Node24.22.1, NestJS12.2.0, existing TypeScript/dependencies/root lock, official CLI65.4.0 and native builder pins. Add `.github/workflows/be-delivery.yml`, narrowly scoped `apps/api/scripts/ci*.ts`, shared `hosted-acceptance.ts` and meaningful tests beside existing API tests; reuse native sealing, strict provider modes, generated migrations, API integration and PostgreSQL/TLS fixtures. The API test command includes the new CI tests; no application data model/public contract/root dependency change is needed. One backend/history, separate FE ownership, evidence-first acceptance and English artifacts satisfy the constitution. Official decisions/pins are in [research](research.md#current-be-cidelivery-decisions--owner-authorized-addon).

### Workflow and artifact contract

- One workflow handles ordinary PRs, main pushes and explicitly dispatched main runs. No path-filtered skipped required check, privileged PR trigger, cross-run PR artifact, dependency cache or persistent checkout credentials. Stable required check: **BE required checks**. Use ephemeral Ubuntu24.04/x64, SHA-pinned official checkout/setup-node/upload/download actions and existing Node24.22.1. The PostgreSQL17.11 runner service uses the official Linux/amd64 digest `sha256:0b81cec82228160abfa753aeaad0609b709eb26eeb950f2e404ad2326e1cc370`, loopback55432 and synthetic fixture credentials only.
- The check job performs unchanged-lock API-only npm ci on its disposable runner, deterministic codegen/migration drift and compilation, builds one native artifact, then runs existing functional/config/HTTP/startup/release/artifact checks plus new CI boundary tests and the real HTTP→test PostgreSQL suite. The integration process receives the exact synthetic `TEST_DATABASE_URL` plus mandatory native artifact/digest inputs; no tsc-only fallback can satisfy native acceptance. PR artifact mode is development; main push/dispatch mode is release.
- Checkout exactly the event SHA. On a trusted main event, materialize only the runner's local main/origin-main refs at that already-published event SHA to satisfy the existing builder; no host-repository Git change. Validate HEAD/tree/repository, clean public source and Node/platform. Deployment additionally checks the real current main ref through GitHub before creation and promotion, so a superseded run cannot promote old code.
- Reuse `buildArtifact`/`verifyArtifact`; empty build runtime environment and isolated public tool bootstrap stay unchanged. Pack the sealed artifact, tested dependency-free compiled delivery helpers and sanitized receipt into a mode-preserving tar archive. The check job emits independent archive/provenance hashes, tree and immutable same-run artifact ID. Deployment verifies the archive hash before extraction/execution and the sealed artifact/source/tree/manifest/file inventory again; it never builds another application artifact.
- Native CI may install public pinned build tools in runner temp; this is not permission to npm-ci/install/prune shared local dependencies. Local verification uses existing Node/dependencies and guarded55432 only. Any unexecuted Linux/GitHub-specific behavior is explicitly limited until a real run.

### Production state machine and schema safety

The **BE production delivery** job needs successful required checks, owner repository variable `BE_DELIVERY_ENABLED=true`, exact repository/main push or main dispatch, first run attempt and `be-production` environment. ### Current official contract and pinned CLI65.4.0

The current [Upload Deployment Files reference](https://vercel.com/docs/rest-api/deployments/upload-deployment-files) specifies `POST /v2/files`, binary `application/octet-stream`, SHA1 integrity via **either `x-vercel-digest` or `x-now-digest`**, `Content-Length`/`x-now-size`, optional teamId/slug and success200, including an already-uploaded file. The existing adapter's endpoint/header/body/status handling matches those documented fields. No evidence supports renaming its digest header, switching to the legacy `/v2/now/files`, or treating this timeout as a proven request-contract mismatch. The page's generated JSON examples are not used instead of its binary request/header contract.

Its uploader streams bytes, emits per-file progress and `file-uploaded` events, permits concurrency and retries transient/server errors with `retries:5,factor:6,minTimeout:10`. That minTimeout is retry backoff, not a transfer deadline. The pinned upload/fetch callsites pass an optional dispatcher and an AbortController used for peer failure; they do not install the project's15s per-request timer. This inspection does not establish one effective wall-clock timeout for every CLI transport/dispatcher, nor prove that increasing the project's deadline would fix the failure.

Content addressing does not grant a safe journal resume. The [creation reference](https://vercel.com/docs/rest-api/deployments/create-a-new-deployment) references previously uploaded files by SHA; [missing_files](https://vercel.com/docs/rest-api/errors#missing-files) is a deployment-creation response. Its separate continuation helper **POSTs `/deployments/{id}/continue`**, requires an existing ID and advises same-ID inspection after an uncertain continuation. ## Current-schema-only and durable release-journal design — 2026-10-30

Trace: FR-014/017/019/020 retained; FR-021–023 and SC-013–015 specify this narrow owner-authorized repair. One BE writer, existing checkout/dependencies/Node24.22.1, one migration history, no runtime/auth/API redesign. Installed prerequisite checks precede existing-plan/task setup with command-local `SPECIFY_FEATURE_DIRECTORY=specs/001-connectivity-foundation SPECIFY_FEATURE_NO_PERSIST=1`; no shared pointer/template replacement or extension hooks. LSP references report no available language server; scoped callsite inspection is the fallback.

### Modes and source gates

`release.ts` keeps ordinary explicitly approved migrations and adds explicit `--current-schema-only`, optional exact `--supersede <owner-approval-json>`, and read-only `--reconcile-run <uuid>`. Current-only and supersession never call `runMigrations`; current history must be initialized, ordered, have no pending/unexpected names, and match the generated immutable checksum manifest and frozen artifact/source binding. Check read-only before uploads and again immediately before create. The database's existing history records names/order, not checksums: checksum proof comes from generated/source/artifact manifests and the bound historical report, not a fabricated DB checksum column. No initialization/adoption/second history.

### Durable evidence and serialization

Add one focused `release-journal.ts` module using existing owner-only/no-symlink filesystem conventions and Node built-ins. Durable exclusive writes use file fsync followed by parent-directory fsync; partial/failed writes remain blocking evidence, never silently deleted for retry. Allocate the new run UUID before intent/receipt/create. Per-run immutable intent, create-intent, acknowledgement and outcome records bind exact run/source/artifact/manifest/target/mode, strict schemas and content hashes. An independently verified source/artifact approval remains the trust anchor; this is tamper detection and conservative recovery, not a signature system against an attacker controlling the owner account.

For controlled supersession, preserve the original root `operator.lock` itself, including inode/value, rather than replacing or deleting it. An exclusive `supersessions/<previous-run>.json` receipt claims exactly one successor and binds the full expected original lock fingerprint, original unknown/null-ID report hash, owner-accepted late-create/alias/HTTPS residual risk and new binding. A separate per-run lock protects the successor. Revalidate originals/receipt/intent/lock before create and acknowledgement. Existing receipt or run identity prevents another execution; no generic unlock/resume-create operation exists. Fresh ordinary releases still use the root exclusive lock. Only a durably recorded safe ordinary outcome permits its matching lock to be released; uncertain migration/create or persistence failure retains it. Supersession evidence/locks remain retained even on success.

### Ordering and failure semantics

1. Verify exact input/source/artifact/migration identity; prepare the verified artifact snapshot. Reserve durable run intent/lock and, when explicitly approved, the exact historical supersession receipt. Refuse any stale or mismatched evidence.
2. Preserve provider preflight, deadlines/cancellation and current-history checks. Ordinary mode alone may execute the existing migration runner and persist its report; current-only records read-only verification without inventing a committed migration attempt.
3. Upload only verified frozen bytes. Immediately before the single create request, recheck source/current history/lock/receipt and durably write create intent. Refuse repeated trigger invocation even after an uncertain response.
4. Validate the provider ID, assign it, synchronously durably record acknowledgement, then and only then inspect. Persistence failure blocks inspection and retains uncertainty/locks. An abort after valid acknowledgement does not skip durable recording. A response arriving after outer cancellation may add immutable acknowledgement evidence without rewriting the earlier failed report.
5. Preserve complete file/source/project/owner/target and same-alias checks around safe authenticated smoke. Persist outcome before any safe ordinary unlock; never label an unknown create terminal. Read-only reconciliation validates the persisted binding/records, checks DB current, and inspects only an already-durable ID; no trigger, migration, lock change or historical-report rewrite. Missing acknowledgement remains unknown even if no candidate appears in a bounded provider window.

Process loss between provider acceptance and local fsync remains fundamentally ambiguous. A durable create intent is permission for at most one invocation by that run, not proof that the provider received it or an exactly-once guarantee. Failed/incomplete fsync, delayed writes, missing/tampered records and lock substitution stop execution; read-only reconciliation must not invent an ID or repair evidence automatically.

### Local verification and external boundary

Extend existing release/integration suites, with synthetic transport that has no network fallback and only random disposable databases on the exact guarded55432 fixture. Cover zero runner/DDL, noncurrent history and checksum/revision rejection; receipt/lock/create/ack/fsync failures and process crashes; delayed/cancelled acknowledgement, ID recovery before inspection, tamper/stale/mismatch refusal, repeated invocation/read-only reconciliation with no second create, and ordinary migration regressions. Exercise the actual driver beyond assertions using a throwaway smoke; reuse unaffected auth/TLS/pool/native evidence rather than rerunning unrelated suites.

After affected checks, retain source hashes and actual results, return the fresh docs lease with protected FE/history evidence, and STOP READY_FOR_REVIEW. Coordinator alone starts one available gpt-6-luna review; confirmed fixes only receive affected rechecks. Later publication requires a fresh serial Git/docs receipt and exact reviewed scope. Build the new clean published Linux/x64 artifact through the unchanged approved public pipeline; no host install/dependency/root-lock change or provenance bypass. External execution remains paused until concrete owner binding to the new commit/tree/artifact/source/platform. That later operation additionally requires fresh bounded full provider/alias/DB-current reconciliation, correlated late-create reconciliation first, and same-deployment authenticated HTTPS evidence; this repair does not assert those external gates passed.

### Authoritative contract and minimal correction

- [Official endpoint documentation](https://vercel.com/docs/rest-api/deployments/list-deployment-files) shows `"mode": "123"` in its JSON example, while its response schema describes `mode` as a number indicating file type and permissions.
- [Current official OpenAPI](https://vercel.com/openapi.json), GET `/v6/deployments/{id}/files`, resolves to `FileTree[]` with `mode:number`, required mode/name/type, optional string uid and recursive directory children. Retrieved document SHA-256: `d307b1d2590addf7814c891dde026140d84d7bb2e9e9868adfdeebe6d64f0765`.
- The [upstream SDK FileTree model](https://github.com/vercel/sdk/blob/main/src/models/filetree.ts) uses `mode: types.number()`. Its [numeric inbound primitive](https://github.com/vercel/sdk/blob/main/src/types/primitives.ts) explicitly converts strings with `Number(x)`. This establishes numeric-string interoperability, not octal interpretation or permission-bit-only equality. The SDK also tolerates other coercions that this release verifier must NOT inherit.
- Preserve numeric integer modes and accept only canonical unsigned decimal strings, bounded to the full16-bit POSIX mode range0–65535. Compare the resulting complete value exactly with `0o100000 | expected.mode`; never strip file-type/special bits, guess octal/hex, trim whitespace, parse prefixes or default missing values. Reject fractions, signs, exponent syntax, leading-zero strings, overflow, arrays/objects/null/booleans and any changed full mode.
- Leave rooted paths, entry type, exact SHA-1 uid equality, duplicate/missing/extra-file rejection, artifact verification, project/deployment/alias binding and migration-before-deploy serialization unchanged. Official uid documentation only calls it a unique file identifier; the actual service's path/root/uid behavior remains a fail-closed provider gate, not inferred proof. No path rebasing, opaque-uid substitution or missing-evidence fallback is authorized.

The correction maps existing FR-017/020 and SC-010 to `apps/api/scripts/vercel-deploy.ts` and `apps/api/tests/release.test.ts`; no new tracker/task IDs or dependency are needed. Extend the existing adapter suite for documented decimal strings and malformed/full-mode/path/type/uid evidence. Run affected compilation/regressions and a throwaway real-adapter loopback transport smoke, without credentials, database access or live provider requests. Stop for ONE coordinator Luna review. Because the adapter is a captured source input, the old Linux artifact becomes stale for this correction; only subsequently reviewed/published exact source and a new clean Linux artifact may proceed, under a coordinated serial Git lease.

## Expanded native artifact and prebuilt design — owner-approved

### Contracts and boundaries

Retain NestJS12.2.0, Node24.22.1, CLI65.4.0 and its unchanged @vercel/nestjs23.0.0/@vercel/node28.0.0 builders. No new runtime dependency, upstream patch, legacy builds, dummy function, framework switch, experimental flag or static cancellation export. Source owns the orchestration and final Build Output API v3 assembly. Existing vercel.json remains the authoritative duration10/cancellation/Git-disable configuration; omit its unsupported functions field only from the isolated upstream builder input and record that transformation. Raw upstream output is evidence, never directly deployable.

Canonical bootstrap: src/main.ts directly uses NestFactory to create the configured application module, applies the existing bearer/CORS composition, listens, and enables safe owned-resource shutdown. src/app.ts owns module/configuration composition without masquerading as a detected native bootstrap. Existing test helpers use the same module/composition; migrate every factory caller, keep transport/application/adapters and the one API contract unchanged. Preserve attachDatabasePool, exact verified Session TLS, hosted max2/local4, all existing deadlines and cancellation.

### Source and artifact construction

Versioned scripts/artifact.ts and scripts/build-artifact.ts use existing Node APIs and public tool code, with an API-workspace entrypoint only. Build requires an explicit destination outside the repository and a new isolated work directory. Copy only enumerated public build inputs (root package/lock, API source/scripts/tests/config/migrations/package/tsconfig/vercel config and the sole contract); never traverse owner .openai/private/auth stores or copy .env/.npmrc. Recheck source contents after capture/build. Git reads, if used by the future source verifier, are read-only; tests use existing archive/synthetic fixtures, never temporary Git commits/indexes.

Tool integrity: use the existing exact pins, not a dependency update. Record official npm SHA-512 tarball integrity for CLI65.4.0/NestJS-builder23.0.0/Node-builder28.0.0/build-utils15.1.0 and compare complete published versus installed own-package inventories, bytes and metadata before tool execution/provenance success. Only a validated installed root node_modules directory is a separately resolved dependency boundary; no nested path or arbitrary file exclusion. Any extra/missing/unsafe/version/content/mode mismatch fails. The supported CLI JavaScript launcher opt-out of the native binary may be used to keep executed CLI code within this verified package; it is not an experimental backend flag. Public tarball reads/extraction remain confined to the isolated work directory.

Source provenance records the approved base revision and Git tree, a sorted file/mode/content manifest and its digest. Development overlays record their actual content separately and are explicitly non-releasable; they are never labeled clean committed source. Future published mode requires exact clean main/source/tree matching the approved published revision, verified again by the release gate. The source config and immutable migration-name/checksum manifest are independently bound.

Run the unchanged native CLI with --prod --non-interactive --standalone, empty HOME/auth/npm configuration and a zero-byte production env file. Public project metadata is the verified relevant subset (project prj_uITHKLUGGE98VxrEcs5FoktBbcls, team team_k2Hk4wx5XAb9Mgc6lP5AjVhf, scope khuonghuyhieu0705s-projects, apps/api/nestjs/Node24); it is not a full genuine pull. No --yes/pull/env pull/authenticated launcher. Record exact tool/builder versions and content integrity, Node/OS/architecture, effective builder input and raw runtime-file manifest. Preserve raw diagnostics separately from the sealed final output. Native dependencies/platform compatibility remain explicit: development host artifacts are not implicitly deployable; published release requires the reviewed Linux build platform and matching function architecture.

The isolated project metadata overrides only the public-copy install command with `npm ci --workspace=@esty-admin/api --include-workspace-root=false`; no Vercel project setting changes. npm user/global configs are separate empty files, telemetry is disabled, and CLI65.4.0's actual CLI-relative bundled public builder packages are compared with pinned official tarballs before execution. The application `functions` config is restored in the public source copy before source-digest revalidation; raw output and invocation evidence stay separate.

Validate upstream output before assembly: exactly one index.func, expected main.js handler and NestJS/Node24 metadata, supported known route shape, empty function environment, no external filePathMap, no extra functions/static/private files and a self-contained dependency/manifest closure. Reject unexpected shapes; do not blindly add controls to arbitrary output. Copy only validated runtime assets into the final artifact, emit the canonical catch-all function route preserving request paths, and explicitly set maxDuration10/supportsCancellation=true on that function. Stage the existing operator/migration assets from the same captured source separately; never upload operator assets or secrets.

Seal a canonical provenance.json with source/config/migration/tool/raw-output evidence and every final/operator file's relative path, type, mode, size, SHA-256 and upload SHA-1. Its SHA-256 is the independently approved artifact identity. Normalize safe read-only file/directory modes; reject traversal, duplicate/case-colliding paths, symlinks/special files, missing/extra files, oversized input and forbidden private/config/environment paths. Require exact target/project/team/Node/platform. Revalidation checks bytes and metadata, not just self-asserted labels. Only provenance.json and the declared output/operator trees are permitted. No original migration asset is rewritten.

### Exact-artifact release

Retire the runnable Git-source deployment trigger. Release CLI requires --artifact and --artifact-sha256 in addition to exact approved commit/migration manifest and separated owner-private contexts; default performs no private read/network operation. Before DB work, verify source publication/cleanliness and artifact identity, then make a private verified snapshot. Retain one operator.lock across verification/status/migration/report/upload/create/identity/domain/smoke. Run the existing operator against that snapshot's original migration assets; do not rebuild source during release.

Use the pinned CLI's prebuilt transport semantics without adding a CLI runtime dependency: upload immutable file bytes to POST /v2/files using the documented content SHA-1 header; create through POST /v13/deployments?prebuilt=1 with exact files/project/production target and no gitSource. Only final .vercel/output contents are uploaded, not source/private/operator context. Query flag semantics are corroborated by CLI65.4.0's public @vercel/client bundle; the general REST create schema omits this flag, so real provider compatibility remains a mandatory external gate.

Exact CLI65.4.0 client wire contract: POST `/v2/files` uses `x-now-digest`, `x-now-size` and Content-Length; successful HTTP200 need not contain JSON. Create-file entries carry full POSIX regular-file mode (`S_IFREG | permissions`), not permissions alone. Synthetic transport hashes received upload bytes, checks lengths and requires those received content addresses before creation. Source comparison corrected the initial header/body/mode assumptions before handoff. This resolves local encoding, not the separate real provider file-UID/attestation gate.

Read and hash bytes before each upload, retain content-addressed upload references, and verify the private snapshot and approved source/artifact again before creation. A post-upload mutation fails before deployment creation; cancellation never retries silently. Return identity is provider id/projectId/ownerId/target/state, bound to this invocation's verified upload receipt, not requested meta. At READY require provider file-tree evidence at GET /v6/deployments/{id}/files to match the exact uploaded paths/modes/content-addressed identifiers, including the sealed artifact evidence. A missing/extra/mismatched or unsupported provider attestation fails closed, never substitutes arbitrary metadata. The documented file UID and pinned upload SHA-1 relationship must be confirmed externally; synthetic tests establish refusal/matching logic, not remote enforcement.

Verify exact project settings/repository/main/no hooks and existing verified domain before migrations. Verify deployment/file evidence plus alias/project/domain before and after safe api.lovora.us HTTP smoke. Preserve conservative locks for unknown/queued/cancelled/mismatched outcomes and DB-unknown state; committed migration plus failed deploy never triggers automatic rollback. No provider API/upload/deploy is executed in this increment; transport is synthetic. Runtime Production env remains owner-managed and excluded from build artifacts.

T061 planning/consistency → T062 bootstrap → T063 upstream build/assembly + T064 integrity → T065 prebuilt release → T066 actual native HTTP + T067 tamper/release tests → T068 evidence/READY_FOR_REVIEW → T059 coordinator-only new Luna review → T060 separately authorized docs-only closeout. One source writer; no speculative parallel writer/reviewer. Original60 IDs/checkmarks and dated failures/reviews remain intact during planning.

Run only affected checks under cached Node24: actual native upstream build, final assembly/inspection, isolated emitted handler outside source/dependency roots, auth/live/unknown/preflight/degraded/recovery, client disconnect and shutdown/resource cleanup. Strict standalone55432 disposable databases only for migration/release/recovery tests; never stop/reset shared stacks or use Supabase-local main/cloud for failure tests. Exercise packaging determinism, controls/handler/routes/closure, source/config/tool/platform mismatches, private/path/symlink exclusion, stale/tampered/missing/extra files, mutation during consumption and synthetic upload ordering/no-Git-rebuild/queued/failure/cancel/uncertain outcomes.

Local HTTP cancellation plus emitted supportsCancellation does not prove a real Vercel worker abort or10-second provider kill. Real platform settings/enforcement, remote file attestation, full genuine metadata, Hobby eligibility, owner inputs, cloud DB preflight/versioned migration, same-artifact deployment/domain/TLS and api.lovora.us+Supabase-cloud acceptance remain external. T054 may record local raw/final native evidence but stays open for those native/provider acceptance limits; no tsc-only substitute. README owns one updated manual list after actual evidence.

## Supabase local setup plan — Story 6

### Goal and boundaries

Preferred development path: local Next.js → local NestJS → local Supabase PostgreSQL. Supabase supplies a local DB and Studio, with supported auxiliary services as needed by the pinned CLI; it does not add Auth/Storage/Realtime application features. A local stack offers a Supabase development environment, not proof of cloud TLS, Session pooler, permissions or Vercel/Render behavior. No remote login/link, db pull/push/reset, remote migrations or cloud account changes.

Reuse installed isolated Supabase CLI2.122.0 and Docker; no npm/global/skill install. Verify the existing launcher/workdir without reading auth stores: its personal cloud project targeting must not bleed into local commands. If needed, add a local-only wrapper/config context that invokes only local CLI operations, clears remote project/token overrides for those child commands and preserves the company/personal cloud launchers unchanged. Do not reinstall or repurpose an authenticated cloud profile.

### Implemented source/configuration layout

- `apps/api/supabase/config.toml`: local project id `esty-admin-local`, PostgreSQL17 major compatible with the hosted direction, explicit non-conflicting local ports and seed/migration replay disabled where supported by pinned CLI. Verify installed CLI schema instead of copying future docs blindly.
- `apps/api/scripts/supabase-local.mjs`: optional thin scoped lifecycle wrapper only if needed for reproducible workdir/isolated local execution. Never call remote commands or invent unsupported flags. Use existing installed CLI rather than downloading another copy.
- `apps/api/package.json`: API-workspace local start/status/stop scripts; preserve existing db:up/db:stop fixture meaning and root FE scripts. Root command additions require a shared-file lease and demonstrated need.
- `apps/api/.env.example`: document a clearly separated Supabase-local application profile, after checking actual synthetic endpoint/credential behavior; never overwrite the owner's existing .env or replace the remote tuple with local values.
- `apps/api/tests/supabase-local.test.ts`: bounded, explicitly local smoke for identity, initial migration state, apply/rerun and actual HTTP readiness. Keep destructive randomized tests on the existing strict standalone fixture.

These files/scripts now exist and were exercised locally. There is no second application migration directory/history. Supabase CLI initializes only its own system schemas/platform metadata; application foundation migrations remain node-pg-migrate in apps/api/migrations with public.foundation_migrations only. Seed/replay are disabled; no application SQL was added to supabase/migrations or applied through Studio.

### Environment and lifecycle

Plan default local endpoints: API gateway127.0.0.1:54321, database127.0.0.1:54322 and Studio127.0.0.1:54323, subject to current port availability and pinned CLI configuration. FE3000/API3001 and standalone fixture55432 remain distinct. Bind stack ports to loopback using the officially supported Docker network/config approach; verify effective bindings rather than assuming defaults are loopback. Identify containers/volumes by the local project id; do not stop unrelated stacks.

Before starting, inspect Docker availability/resources, CLI compatibility, occupied ports and existing local project state. Image downloads/resource consumption must be disclosed as part of the approved local setup; do not mutate shared npm dependencies. Inspect the resolved config and generated filenames; keep runtime state/.temp/build outputs ignored and exclude credentials from staged files. Retrieve only sanitized local connection metadata, never dump CLI status output containing keys/tokens. Use synthetic local DB values in the documented app profile; no cloud password or service-role key is required for Nest's pg connection.

Start the approved local stack; verify service versions and health. Preserve existing standalone fixture volume; it can remain idle/stopped if needed for resources, but do not remove it. Startup must not apply application migrations automatically. Before explicit app migration execution, inspect foundation/history presence; on a fresh approved local project apply the existing operator once then again with expected1→0, or compatible current0→0. Stop on unexpected schema/history rather than reset/adopt. Do not run db reset, down -v, remote SQL or cleanup that deletes persisted local data.

Use the actual loopback DB URL with API_RUNTIME=local and the existing local CORS origin. No fake hosted marker, preview guard or real Supabase secret is needed. For failure/recovery, isolate the test at the client/process configuration boundary or only a newly created disposable resource that the test owns; do not stop the entire Supabase stack or change shared system schemas to simulate failure. ## Versioned migrations and release visibility — owner-confirmed policy

Retain the implemented migration versions, failure attribution and sanitized report interface. A future approved release automatically runs the same serialized operator before deployment; this adaptation does not perform real SQL or alter migration assets/history.

### Existing mechanisms to retain

Current source already requires unique13-digit timestamp-name.mjs filenames, generates ordered name/SHA-256 metadata and uses public.foundation_migrations for applied name/time history. Example existing version:1792972800000-foundation. The runner enforces ordering, advisory locking and a single transaction. Do not create a second v1/v2 numbering sequence or application migration history.

Existing reporting distinguishes attempts, transaction acknowledgements and report-write failure from committed history. Preserve it rather than reinstating count-only output. Packaged checksums are not a database-stored historical checksum ledger; do not claim tamper detection beyond the inspected assets/history.

### Retained status and report contract

- A read-only local migration-status command compares the approved ordered manifest with applied history and reports applied/pending/missing-or-unexpected versions. An absent history on an approved fresh target means not initialized/pending; status must not create it. Use v9-supported APIs or explicit read-only history queries, not a newer-version status API assumed available.
- Each attempt gets run_id, optional source revision/release id, explicit non-secret target label, manifest digest, start/end timestamps, duration, before/after committed versions, attempted version list and result. Per-version names/checksums derive from the manifest. Do not log DB URLs/passwords, raw SQL/driver messages/stacks or business values.
- Report stage: config, assets, connect, lock/order, migration, commit or verification. For errors tied to a migration, record its exact version. For pre-execution/load/commit errors without a reliable per-file attribution, report the stage and version unknown/not_started instead of guessing the final pending file.
- Use verified v9 loader/logger instrumentation to retain version context through migration action generation and SQL execution; allowlist known manifest names, never forward raw upstream logger/error text. Tests must demonstrate attribution for both a thrown migration function and a failing SQL statement in a later migration. No per-file success is committed until the shared transaction actually commits.
- Distinguish attempted/executed_uncommitted, applied_committed, rolled_back and not_started. If the batch rolls back, all attempted migrations in that transaction remain unapplied in DB history; the external sanitized report records the failed attempt. If rollback or final verification is uncertain, report unknown and stop, not a successful recovery.
- CLI emits a concise sanitized summary, returns nonzero on failure and writes a machine-readable local report under ignored .openai/reports/migrations/<run_id>.json. Future CI retains a sanitized report as a release artifact, not a second database ledger or new monitoring service. Local report write failure must be surfaced without claiming the DB rolled back after it already committed; record/reporting errors and DB outcome are separate.
- Already-applied files are immutable; introduce a new uniquely named version for each schema change and regenerate the manifest. Duplicate timestamps, invalid names, missing applied versions/order mismatch and package/manifest drift block execution. Do not claim tamper detection for historic DB checksums unless a prior trusted report/manifest actually exists.

### Local workflow and future release workflow

Local: author new migration → generate/check manifest → inspect status → run explicit local migration command → inspect summary/status → rerun unchanged expecting0 new applications. App startup remains independent. Validate failure and transactional rollback only in disposable fixture databases, preserving shared Supabase-local system/data state.

Future release: approved exact target/source artifact → build/tests/asset validation → one serialized operator migration job → verify committed history and save sanitized report → deploy API → smoke/readiness checks. Migration failure/nonzero exit blocks deployment. A deployment failure after a committed migration does not undo the database automatically; inspect compatibility and use a separately reviewed forward fix or rollback. Never promise zero downtime: current readiness checks the entire ordered migration set, so an older process can report pending/degraded after the database advances. Review expand/contract and rollout compatibility before enabling a hosted pipeline.

Keep operator credentials separate from API runtime. Vercel adaptation replaces only provider assumptions around the existing runner/report interface; no new provider, paid CI or migration permission is implied. Do not add DDL to API startup/build/install.

Version-matched reference: [node-pg-migrate v9 API](https://github.com/salsita/node-pg-migrate/blob/v9.0.0/docs/src/api.md). Dated executed report tests below remain the unchanged baseline.

Diagnostics: distinguish configuration, stack/port, database-connectivity, migration/history and API/HTTP failures. Emit timestamp, operation/run id, safe stage/version where known, duration/result and next actionable local step. Never emit full DB URLs/passwords/keys/raw SQL/driver stacks. Keep concise terminal summaries and existing ignored local report paths; no Sentry, paid logging service, dashboard, notification integration or automatic background polling is required.

Local acceptance: verified CLI/config/project id and loopback ports; reproducible start/status/stop/restart preserving volumes; actual local API live/readiness with the single committed migration history; unchanged rerun0; precise sanitized disposable-fixture migration failure reports; relevant config/HTTP/integration regressions; one scoped independent Luna review for the actual implementation increment. Full local foundation acceptance additionally requires coordinated FE T014–T016. Documentation must distinguish recorded execution from proposed commands and report blockers honestly.

Proposed screen route: `/connectivity`, separate from `/application`'s reference frame and public home/privacy. For the local-only browser target, allow exactly the configured loopback HTTP API origin, default127.0.0.1:3001; do not accept a query-param/user-entered arbitrary host. FE uses its existing loopback origin, default127.0.0.1:3000, and Nest CORS must match it exactly. E2E may use explicitly configured non-conflicting loopback ports for processes it owns. No DB URL/key/password is sent to the browser, and no Supabase browser SDK is required for this pg-based connectivity check.

### Screen and interaction contract

| Field/control | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| API process | Result of GET /health/live;200 with contract status ok confirms process only |
| Database readiness | Result of GET /health/ready;200 requires database up and migrations current |
| Degraded reason |503 maps database_unavailable/database_timeout/migrations_pending to clear safe copy |
| Check connection | User-triggered fresh check of both endpoints; initial screen is idle, no fake success |
| Last checked | Local completion time of the current attempt; do not present a historical ready result as current |
| Retry | Same action after failure; keyboard accessible with announced state |

States: idle → checking → ready/degraded/unreachable; process-live and database-ready are distinct indicators. During a new check, clear stale success and disable duplicate submissions. No automatic polling or retry loop. Bound requests with an explicit client timeout (proposed5s, longer than the existing3000ms handler deadline); cancel on unmount and prevent an older response overwriting a newer state. A successful liveness response plus readiness503 must show API running/DB degraded. Network/CORS/invalid-response cases show a safe reachability/response error without inventing a database reason;503 without a valid contract body is an invalid response, not ready.

No business records, shop connection, authentication, fixture CRUD or operational dashboard metrics. Provide semantic headings, status/live announcement, visible focus and 360px/390px/1440px readability using existing UI skills. Error copy must not print URLs with credentials, raw driver errors or arbitrary response bodies.

### Real local browser acceptance

Use the existing production Playwright suite plus focused state/transport checks only where they add distinct value. Extend rather than duplicate the suite. Main journey: default-disabled route404 → explicitly enabled idle screen → check against actual local Nest/Supabase → API/live and DB/migration readiness → retry after failure → fresh recovery. Record the configuration used, ports, actual results and limitations.

| Case | Isolation and expected result |
| --- | --- |
| Real success | Test-owned API uses the already-approved/migrated Supabase-local app target; browser sees live200 and ready200 through the generated client |
| API unavailable | Stop only the test-owned API process, retry and show unreachable; leave owner's dev servers/stack alone |
| DB unavailable | Restart only the test-owned API with an unreachable synthetic loopback DB endpoint; live remains200 and ready503; no Supabase stop/schema mutation |
| Recovery | Restore test-owned API configuration/process and retry; show current real readiness, never cached success |
| Pending history | If required, use a separately owned disposable app target with explicit local scope; never alter/reset the shared Supabase main database/history to force this case |
| UI/regressions | Keyboard/status/timeout/stale-response behavior; mobile reflow; original home/privacy/legacy redirect/404/Application journeys remain passing |

Tests use only their own isolated ports/processes; never reuse/kill owner servers. No existing destructive integration suite is redirected to Supabase. If the approved local stack is unavailable, report a concrete blocker rather than passing from intercepted/fake success. Transport-only failure mocks can test boundaries but do not establish the real success journey.

### Handoff sequence and exit

1. To do BE implements only the approved Supabase-local setup slice and returns actual endpoint/config/contract evidence, with no FE writes.
2. Owner authorizes the separate FE T014–T016 handoff and any needed install window. FE owns its client/screen/browser files; negotiate shared scripts/docs before editing.
3. OMP implements scoped FE behavior and checks; Codex obtains one concise read-only Luna code review after execution and presents the local screen/evidence for owner acceptance.
4. Update task/evidence status truthfully. Local UI→API→Supabase proof completes only applicable local criteria; all provider/native/real-cloud/deployment/Etsy gates remain pending. Commit/push is a separate owner request.

Default route/flag/timeout are review proposals, not installed config or approved new runtime behavior. Keep this detail in feature001 rather than creating a new feature/tracker.

### A2 — Ordered activation and the minimal bridge

Recommended engineering choice: a **bounded two-history bridge**, followed by strict target-only app admission. No owner architecture question is needed for this conservative default. A separately elected maintenance window is the alternative in A4.

Implement a small immutable transition definition in proposed `apps/api/src/config/schema-transition.ts`, containing exactly OLD/TARGET and their ordered entries. Extend the existing config/probe/composition in `apps/api/src/config/config.ts`, `apps/api/src/connectivity/adapters/postgres.ts` and `apps/api/src/app.ts`. Proposed `APP_SCHEMA_MODE` has only `strict` (default) and `activation-bridge`. It is a new field to implement/test, not a setting the current binary already supports.

Bridge mode requires `APP_FOUNDATION_ENABLED=false`; reject bridge+enabled app at configuration time. The bridge mounts only the existing health controllers, keeps technical bearer/origin protection before DB work, and classifies readiness as compatible only for the exact OLD or exact TARGET sequence. The OLD entry must match TARGET's prefix and compiled immutable manifest. Do not allow every prefix: the two-entry intermediate is rejected because the two application migrations run in one transaction. Missing, duplicate, reordered, foreign/future history and altered assets fail closed. Strict mode retains exact TARGET comparison. No runtime-provided history list, wildcard, `>=` migration count or disabled check is permitted.

The bridge artifact still honestly packages TARGET's three files and generated manifest. Its separate sealed approval record says that disabled-app bridge readiness supports OLD/TARGET; it does not relabel TARGET as OLD or change current-only CI's pin. A bridge health `current` response means current under this bounded foundation-only policy, not that application migrations/Owner admission are complete. Candidate/final receipts must explicitly carry the phase and observed exact history. HTTP response DTOs remain canonical0.4.0.

The same clean Linux artifact can be deployed twice with separately approved configuration snapshots: first disabled-app bridge using the existing foundation runtime identity/configuration, then strict app mode using `app_runtime`. Existing foundation credentials are not exported into the new operator packet. Bridge mode cannot expose app controllers even if application code is bundled. An app fallback to the bridge is an announced app outage, not a successful app rollback.

| Stage | Required action after its own authorization | Exit evidence / next-stage fence |
|---|---|---|
| S0 Freeze/review/publish/build | Approve decisions, implement, run local checks, one scoped review, serial publication, clean Linux artifact and FE source freeze | Exact source/tree/artifact/contract/OLD/TARGET/configuration phase; no private inputs during build |
| S1 Reserve/read-only preflight | Reserve durable activation intent and production serialization; confirm target/TLS/exact OLD, existing predecessor, absent-or-exact roles, migration authority, recovery/backup prerequisites | No SQL mutation; unknown prior intent or mismatched target/history/role attributes stops |
| S2 Compatible predecessor | Create one disabled-app bridge candidate from the approved TARGET artifact, attest files/config phase, test OLD compatibility/denial, promote and bind final API hostname | Known bridge deployment ID and actual final-host proof before any role/schema mutation |
| S3 Provision/migrate | Record role intent; provision exact approved roles; verify role outcome; record migration intent and run existing runner once against exact OLD→TARGET | Durable confirmed commit/report plus fresh exact TARGET/ACL/definer evidence; no deploy on failed/unknown result |
| S4 Bootstrap | Record input/approval binding and bootstrap intent; invoke existing verified-user bootstrap once with separated operator inputs | Exact expected workspace/Owner/audit state and confirmed outcome; matching replay is only a reconciled approved action |
| S5 Strict app BE | Supply approved app-runtime variables, create one strict app candidate from the same tested artifact, attest files/current schema/admission, promote and verify same deployment at `https://api.lovora.us` | Strict TARGET, `app_runtime`, real current-session/capability admission and technical denials; bridge remains the only schema-compatible foundation fallback |
| S6 FE and hosted journey | FE OMP/release coordinator build exact published FE source as a held Production candidate configured for the final `APP_ORIGIN`; verify source/build/configuration and bounded noncredential checks, durably promote that known candidate without rebuilding, then run authenticated acceptance at the final origin | Actual final-origin HTTPS browser→BFF→Auth/Nest→Supabase proof and unchanged public routes; no generated-host login or local/provider artifact-equivalence claim |
| S7 Closeout | Observe bounded health/redacted errors, retain receipts, remove only explicitly released synthetic test resources, return exact leases | Actual acceptance or one precise fenced blocker; no automatic rollback, cleanup of manual006, or production destructive test |

The dependency order after the compatibility prerequisite is role preflight/provisioning → migration → bootstrap → BE → FE → HTTPS. FE publication/build gating must be coordinated before any source push so it cannot bypass S5. A native FE provider build is identified by its actual deployment/source/build record, not by a local `.next` digest. Keep the approved final `APP_ORIGIN` fixed: a generated candidate hostname is not interchangeable with the exact Origin/Host/cookie binding. Prepromotion checks are source/build/configuration and bounded noncredential checks only; perform the complete browser session journey after durable promotion at the configured final origin. Do not spoof Host, relax origins, change configuration or rebuild to manufacture prepromotion login proof. A failed final-origin journey leaves acceptance fenced for explicit compatible recovery, not an automatic rollback or successful release claim.

### A3 — Durable orchestration, secure boundaries and reconciliation

Implement proposed `apps/api/scripts/activation.ts` around existing migration/bootstrap/release functions; extend `ci-delivery.ts`, `ci-provider.ts`, relevant release helpers and `.github/workflows/be-delivery.yml` only where necessary. Reuse existing GitHub production-environment approval/concurrency and durable deployment intent machinery; do not add a service, DB ledger, queue or second migration runner. The old current-only mode remains unchanged and runner-free. Activation requires its own exact approved transition packet; merely changing `approvedMigrationDigest` is forbidden.

The packet binds source commit/tree, artifact digest, OLD/TARGET entries and digests, predecessor/bridge identity, target, phase, reviewed code/check/review receipts and a private bootstrap-input binding. Public reports contain safe phase/outcome/hash/count data, never Auth identity/workspace UUIDs, email, DSNs, passwords, admin/technical/bypass keys or cookies. Store sensitive approved input bindings only in the later authorized private channel; the public placeholder is `OWNER_AUTH_USER_ID`.

Use one release lock spanning S1–S6; current-only and activation modes share the same production exclusion. Keep `cancel-in-progress: false` semantics and reject automatic reruns/overlapping unresolved attempts. The existing runner keeps its advisory lock, ordered-history verification and `singleTransaction: true`; bootstrap keeps `pg_advisory_xact_lock(5005,1)` and its transaction. Locks alone are not crash evidence: durable intent must precede each role/migration/bootstrap/create/promotion side effect. Persist confirmed outcomes before advancing and fsync local receipts as already established. An unavailable durable journal means zero next side effects.

Each bridge/app deployment phase has its own one-shot create fence; two intentional stages are not permission to retry an unknown create. Persist a valid acknowledged deployment ID before inspect, even if the CLI exits nonzero. Bind candidate files, source, region `icn1`, configuration phase and predecessor before and after inspection/promotion. Reconcile only an already known ID; if acknowledgement is absent, use bounded read-only provider evidence to establish identity, never create another candidate to find out.

| Observed state | Required stop/recovery rule |
|---|---|
| Role provisioning not started or transaction confirmed rolled back | Keep bridge and journal; inspect roles/history read-only and approve a new attempt explicitly |
| Role commit unknown or roles partially/mismatched provisioned | Fence migration; compare exact role attributes/memberships/ACL outcome with the approved role packet; do not drop/reset/regrant existing roles blindly |
| Migration rolled back, exact OLD restored | No bootstrap/app deploy; retain failure/report and bridge; repair/review cause before an explicitly approved attempt |
| Migration committed and exact TARGET verified | Never rerun DDL because a later phase failed; continue only from the confirmed durable stage after approval |
| Migration COMMIT acknowledgement lost or report persistence failed | Mark unknown/unresolved even if process exit looks successful; read exact history and expected app objects/function/ACLs using approved authority; append reconciliation outcome before any continuation |
| Two-entry history, unexpected objects or other partial state | Not a successful transaction and not an acceptable bridge prefix; stop and investigate against original intent/source; no automatic “finish the rest,” history edit or down |
| Bootstrap timeout/unknown commit | Do not call bootstrap again blindly; read expected workspace, active Owner, bootstrap approval/fingerprint and audit tuple using operator authority; classify exact committed, exact absent or conflict |
| SQL/bootstrap committed, app create/promotion failed | Keep compatible bridge/known candidate, preserve data and intent; resolve known deployment/configuration only, no699 rollback or bootstrap-as-repair |
| Create/promotion outcome unknown | Fence both BE and FE publication/promotion continuation; read binding/known ID and journal, never clear the intent or start a second create automatically |

Separate phase processes/environments so source/tool preparation sees no credentials, migration sees only its approved DB authority, bootstrap sees only its operator DB/Auth inputs, and provider create sees only provider/technical acceptance credentials. Explicit allowlists, bounded execution, private0700/0600 handoff where needed and safe error classification are required. No credentials in command arguments, workflow artifacts/cache, child stdout or general environment dumps. Existing CI's three provider secrets do not magically supply migration/bootstrap credentials: adding those later needs explicit protected-environment approval or a separately approved private operator invocation of the same orchestrator. Do not discover local owner files or reuse account caches.

### A4 — Cutover, backup and rollback limits

The recommended bridge avoids knowingly breaking the old readiness contract when TARGET commits. Existing699 exact-history readiness rejects added app migrations; never run live SQL first and call its resulting503 a harmless warning. Test bridge OLD/TARGET compatibility before approving its real deployment and verify its real binding before S3.

If the owner rejects the bridge, the only alternative is a separately approved bounded maintenance cutover: name the window/start/end/abort owner and affected app/API routes, freeze automated deployments, keep public FE pages available, verify backup/recovery prerequisites, announce readiness unavailability, then execute the same serialized migration/bootstrap/strict BE/FE sequence. The owner must accept that the old API may stay unavailable after SQL commits if deployment fails. No invented zero-downtime promise, open-ended window or implicit maintenance approval.

Before live mutation, verify the existing provider's actual backup/export availability, recovery procedure and last usable restore point through separately authorized evidence. Define the acceptable recovery point/time with the owner if restore is the elected recovery mechanism; no paid backup/PITR assumption. A database restore can lose post-snapshot app/Auth changes and invalidate session assumptions; a schema-only dump is not a complete managed Auth backup. Do not copy managed Auth fixture DDL or restore over the live project as a test.

After TARGET commits,699 is not a valid rollback. Prefer fixing configuration or promoting a known compatible tested artifact; a disabled-app bridge is only a health-level holding state. Vercel rollback reuses the old deployment/configuration and does not undo DB/Auth or adopt new project variables. Check actual rollback eligibility; documentation alone does not establish project entitlement. No automatic down, destructive reset, workspace/role deletion or rollback across an incompatible schema. Preserve original failure and acknowledgement evidence.

### A5 — Least privilege and managed Auth compatibility

Proposed `apps/api/scripts/app-roles.ts` performs reviewed, approval-bound role preflight/provisioning, not fixture replay. Fixed role names are `app_runtime` and `app_operator`; use distinct secure passwords/Session usernames, `LOGIN`, `NOINHERIT`, `NOSUPERUSER`, `NOCREATEDB`, `NOCREATEROLE`, `NOREPLICATION`, `NOBYPASSRLS`, and no privileged memberships/role-switch path. Verify actual effective privileges, not only four flags. Existing mismatched roles stop; do not silently alter passwords/ownership. This operator step creates roles before0001's conditional grants; it does not run under `app_operator`, which cannot create roles/schema.

Migration authority is separate from the bootstrap operator and runtime. The approved existing managed migration owner must be able to create the app-owned objects and own the exact definer with actual managed Auth visibility. Keep managed Auth ownership/RLS unchanged; never grant runtime/operator `SELECT auth.sessions`, `BYPASSRLS`, superuser or blanket schema/table access. `app_operator` gets only0001's bootstrap/reconciliation grants, not ongoing runtime/DDL authority.
